This feature owns the\ntext rendering used by every command so that the format is defined once.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `cli.output.render_csv(header: list[str], rows: list[list[str]]) -> str` returns the header line\n  followed by one line per row, each terminated by `\\n`. Fields are joined with `,` with no quoting\n  and no padding.\n- `cli.output.render_diagnostic(message: str) -> str` returns the message followed by a single `\\n`.\n  The caller writes the result to standard error.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `render_csv` never alters a field value. None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nRule 6 of the cash ledger. A date is accepted only when it is a real calendar date in the form\n`YYYY-MM-DD`. This feature\nprovides the one wrapper that every mutating command and every load runs inside.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `cli.transactional.run_command(db, command)` calls `command(store)` inside one database\n  transaction, where `store` is the `db` handle. When `command` returns normally, the transaction\n  commits and the return value of `command` is returned. When `command` raises, the transaction\n  rolls back and the exception is re-raised unchanged.\n- Callers inside `command` use the declared operations, for example\n  `store.accounts.create(account=..., name=...)`, with keyword arguments named by the Input DTO\n  fields in `DATABASE.md`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- The wrapper adds no locking. Concurrent processes rely on the database's own transaction\n  isolation.\n- Rollback covers every table written inside the command, not only the last one.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC integ-txn-commit-on-success ===\nIntent: run_command commits the writes of a command that returns normally.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom cli.transactional import run_command\n\ncode = \"CASH1\"\nname = \"Float\"\nwith db_session() as db:\n    def succeeding(store):\n        store.accounts.create(account=code, name=name)\n        return None\n\n    run_command(db, succeeding)\n    rows = db.accounts.list()\n    assert len(rows) == 1\n    assert rows[0].account == code\n    assert rows[0].name == name\n=== END AC integ-txn-commit-on-success ===\n\n=== AC integ-txn-rollback-single-table ===\nIntent: run_command rolls back a write made before the command raises, leaving the table empty.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom cli.transactional import run_command\n\ncode = \"CASH1\"\nname = \"Float\"\nwith db_session() as db:\n    def failing(store):\n        store.accounts.create(account=code, name=name)\n        raise ValueError(\"rejected after write\")\n\n    raised = False\n    try:\n        run_command(db, failing)\n    except ValueError:\n        raised = True\n    assert raised\n    assert len(db.accounts.list()) == 0\n=== END AC integ-txn-rollback-single-table ===\n\n=== AC integ-txn-rollback-multi-table ===\nIntent: run_command rolls back writes to two tables when the command raises after both writes.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom cli.transactional import run_command\n\ncode = \"CASH1\"\nname = \"Float\"\ncategory = \"OPENING\"\ndescription = \"Opening balance\"\nwith db_session() as db:\n    def failing(store):\n        store.accounts.create(account=code, name=name)\n        store.categories.create(category=category, description=description)\n        raise ValueError(\"rejected after two writes\")\n\n    raised = False\n    try:\n        run_command(db, failing)\n    except ValueError:\n        raised = True\n    assert raised\n    assert len(db.accounts.list()) == 0\n    assert len(db.categories.list()) == 0\n=== END AC integ-txn-rollback-multi-table ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- A command that fails leaves every table exactly as it was before the command started.\n- No application-level lock is added around the transaction.\n- Database drivers are never opened here; access goes through the persistence handle only.\n=== END ARTIFACT BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-CliOutput.md ===\n# FEATURE: CSV Output\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Render successful command output as CSV lines and failure diagnostics as one stderr line. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CliEntry.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | render_csv, render_diagnostic |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nEvery successful command except `init` writes CSV to standard output: one header row, then data\nrows, comma-separated, no quoting, no trailing spaces, and `\\n` line endings. A failing command\nwrites nothing to standard output and one diagnostic line to standard error. This feature owns the\ntext rendering used by every command so that the format is defined once.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `cli.output.render_csv(header: list[str], rows: list[list[str]]) -> str` returns the header line\n  followed by one line per row, each terminated by `\\n`. Fields are joined with `,` with no quoting\n  and no padding.\n- `cli.output.render_diagnostic(message: str) -> str` returns the message followed by a single `\\n`.\n  The caller writes the result to standard error.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `render_csv` never alters a field value. Callers validate fields before rendering.\n- With an empty `rows` list, only the header line is returned.\n- `init` writes nothing; it does not call `render_csv`.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC cli-output-csv-shape ===\nIntent: render_csv writes the header and each row as one comma-separated line, in order.\n\nfrom cli.output import render_csv\n\nheader = [\"account\", \"name\"]\nrows = [[\"DRAWER1\", \"Front Drawer\"], [\"SAFE\", \"Store Safe\"]]\ntext = render_csv(header, rows)\nassert [line.split(\",\") for line in text.split(\"\\n\")[:-1]] == [header] + rows\nassert text.count(\"\\n\") == len(rows) + 1\nassert text.split(\"\\n\")[-1] == \"\"\n=== END AC cli-output-csv-shape ===\n\n=== AC cli-output-csv-header-only ===\nIntent: render_csv with no rows writes only the header line.\n\nfrom cli.output import render_csv\n\nheader = [\"table\", \"rows\"]\ntext = render_csv(header, [])\nassert [line.split(\",\") for line in text.split(\"\\n\")[:-1]] == [header]\nassert text.count(\"\\n\") == 1\n=== END AC cli-output-csv-header-only ===\n\n=== AC cli-output-csv-no-padding ===\nIntent: render_csv adds no leading or trailing spaces to any line.\n\nfrom cli.output import render_csv\n\nheader = [\"table\", \"rows\"]\nrows = [[\"accounts\", \"4\"], [\"categories\", \"7\"]]\ntext = render_csv(header, rows)\nassert all(line == line.strip() for line in text.split(\"\\n\"))\n=== END AC cli-output-csv-no-padding ===\n\n=== AC cli-output-diagnostic-one-line ===\nIntent: render_diagnostic returns the message as exactly one line.\n\nfrom cli.output import render_diagnostic\n\nmessage = \"account DRAWER9 not found\"\ntext = render_diagnostic(message)\nassert text.rstrip(\"\\n\") == message\nassert text.count(\"\\n\") == 1\n=== END AC cli-output-diagnostic-one-line ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Standard output carries CSV only; diagnostics never go to standard output.\n- Field values are never quoted, escaped, or padded by this feature.\n- Money is rendered by the amount formatter, not by this feature.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-ValidateText.md ===\n# FEATURE: Text Validation\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Validate account and category codes, names, descriptions, and memos. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CliEntry.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | check_code, check_name, check_memo |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nRules 3 and 4 of the cash ledger: codes, names, descriptions, and memos are checked before any\ncommand writes to the database. A violation is reported by the command layer with exit code 3.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `cli.text_validation.check_code(text: str) -> str` returns `text` when it is 1 to 12 characters\n  from `A-Z` and `0-9`; otherwise raises `ValueError`.\n- `cli.text_validation.check_name(text: str) -> str` returns `text` when it is 1 to 40 characters\n  and contains no comma and no newline; otherwise raises `ValueError`. Used for names and\n  descriptions.\n- `cli.text_validation.check_memo(text: str) -> str` returns `text` when it is 0 to 60 characters\n  and contains no comma and no newline; otherwise raises `ValueError`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- Validators never normalize case or whitespace; a lowercase code is rejected.\n- Validators do not touch the database.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC valid-text-code-accepts ===\nIntent: check_code returns each 1-to-12-character A-Z/0-9 code unchanged.\n\nfrom cli.text_validation import check_code\n\nsamples = [\"A\", \"AB12\", \"Z9\", \"A\" * 12]\naccepted = [check_code(code) for code in samples]\nassert accepted == samples\n=== END AC valid-text-code-accepts ===\n\n=== AC valid-text-code-rejects ===\nIntent: check_code raises ValueError for empty, lowercase, punctuated, spaced, and over-length codes.\n\nfrom cli.text_validation import check_code\n\nsamples = [\"\", \"ab\", \"A-1\", \"A B\", \"A\" * 13]\nrejected = []\nfor code in samples:\n    try:\n        check_code(code)\n    except ValueError:\n        rejected.append(code)\nassert rejected == samples\n=== END AC valid-text-code-rejects ===\n\n=== AC valid-text-name-accepts ===\nIntent: check_name returns each 1-to-40-character name without comma or newline unchanged.\n\nfrom cli.text_validation import check_name\n\nsamples = [\"N\", \"Front Drawer\", \"N\" * 40]\naccepted = [check_name(name) for name in samples]\nassert accepted == samples\n=== END AC valid-text-name-accepts ===\n\n=== AC valid-text-name-rejects ===\nIntent: check_name raises ValueError for empty, over-length, comma-bearing, and newline-bearing names.\n\nfrom cli.text_validation import check_name\n\nsamples = [\"\", \"N\" * 41, \"a,b\", \"a\\nb\"]\nrejected = valid-text-memo-accepts ===\nIntent: check_memo returns a 0-to-60-character memo without comma or newline unchanged, including an empty memo.\n\nfrom cli.text_validation import check_memo\n\nsamples = [\"\", \"walk in sale\", \"m\" * 60]\naccepted = [check_memo(memo) for memo in samples]\nassert accepted == samples\n=== END AC valid-text-memo-accepts ===\n\n=== AC valid-text-memo-rejects valid-text-memo-rejects ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- A validator never accepts a value the Rules section rejects.\n- Validators raise ValueError only; the command layer maps the failure to exit code 3.\n- Validators never read or write the database.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-ValidateAmount.md ===\n# FEATURE: Amount Validation\n\n| Field Amounts are stored as integer cents. Input text matching\n`-?DIGITS[.D[D]]` is parsed without floating point; output always has exactly two decimals.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `cli.amount.parse_amount(text: str) -> int` returns the signed integer number of cents. It accepts\n  text matching `-?DIGITS[.D[D]]` with at most two decimal places and a non-zero value; otherwise it\n  raises `ValueError`.\n- `cli.amount.format_amount(cents: int) -> str` returns the amount with exactly two decimal places,\n  a leading `-` when negative, and no thousands separator.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- Parsing uses integer arithmetic only.\n- A single decimal place is padded to two places in the stored value, not in the input.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC valid-amount-parse-cents ===\nIntent: parse_amount converts accepted input text to the expected integer cents.\n\nfrom cli.amount import parse_amount\n\ncases = [(\"12.5\", 1250), (\"-0.05\", -5), (\"7\", 700), (\"0.1\", 10), (\"-3\", -300), (\"1.10\", 110)]\nparsed = [parse_amount(text) for text, _ in cases]\nassert parsed == [cents for _, cents in cases]\n=== END AC valid-amount-parse-cents ===\n\n=== AC valid-amount-round-trip ===\nIntent: format_amount of parse_amount returns each canonical two-decimal input unchanged.\n\nfrom cli.amount import format_amount, parse_amount\n\nsamples = [\"12.50\", \"-0.05\", \"0.10\", \"5000.00\", \"-12.50\"]\nassert [format_amount(parse_amount(text)) for text in samples] == samples\n=== END AC valid-amount-round-trip ===\n\n=== AC valid-amount-rejects ===\nIntent: parse_amount raises ValueError for zero, excess decimals, malformed, signed-plus, and empty input.\n\nfrom cli.amount import parse_amount\n\nsamples = [\"0\", \"0.00\", \"-0\", \"1.234\", \"1.\", \".5\", \"1,00\", \"+5\", \"abc\", \"\"]\nrejected = []\nfor text in samples:\n    try:\n valid-amount-integer-type ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- No floating-point value is used for money at any layer.\n- Zero amounts are rejected on input.\n- Output never 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Accept only real calendar dates written YYYY-MM-DD. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CliEntry.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | check_date |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nRule 6 of the cash ledger. A date is accepted only when it is a real calendar date in the form\n`YYYY-MM-DD`. Impossible dates such as `2026-02-30` are rejected.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `cli.dates.check_date(text: str) -> str` returns `text` when it is a real calendar date in the form\n  `YYYY-MM-DD`; otherwise raises `ValueError`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- The check uses the calendar, including leap years.\n- Only the exact `YYYY-MM-DD` shape is accepted; single-digit months or days are rejected.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC valid-date-accepts ===\nIntent: check_date returns each real calendar date in YYYY-MM-DD form unchanged, including a leap day.\n\nfrom cli.dates import check_date\n\nsamples = [\"2026-01-01\", \"2024-02-29\", \"2026-12-31\"]\nassert [check_date(text) for text in samples] == samples\n=== END AC valid-date-accepts ===\n\n=== AC valid-date-rejects ===\nIntent: check_date raises ValueError for impossible dates and for every malformed shape.\n\nfrom cli.dates import check_date\n\nsamples = [\n    \"2026-02-30\",\n    \"2025-02-29\",\n    \"2026-13-01\",\n    \"2026-00-10\",\n    \"2026-04-31\",\n    \"2026-1-01\",\n    \"26-01-01\",\n    \"2026/01/01\",\n    \"\",\n]\nrejected = []\nfor text in samples:\n    try:\n        check_date(text)\n    except ValueError:\n        rejected.append(text)\nassert rejected == samples\n=== END AC valid-date-rejects ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- A date that the calendar does not contain is never accepted.\n- Dates are never converted to another format by this feature.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-CommandTransaction.md ===\n# FEATURE: Command Transaction\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Run each command as one database transaction that commits on success and rolls back on any failure. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CliEntry.md, DATABASE.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | run_command |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nRule 1 of the cash ledger: every command either completes in full or changes nothing. This feature\nprovides the one wrapper that every mutating command and every load runs inside.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `cli.transactional.run_command(db, command)` calls `command(store)` inside one database\n  transaction, where `store` is the `db` handle. When `command` returns normally, the transaction\n  commits and the return value of `command` is returned. When `command` raises, the transaction\n  rolls back and the exception is re-raised unchanged.\n- Callers inside `command` use the declared operations, for example\n  `store.accounts.create(account=..., name=...)`, with keyword arguments named by the Input DTO\n  fields in `DATABASE.md`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- The wrapper adds no locking. Concurrent processes rely on the database's own transaction\n  isolation.\n- Rollback covers every table written inside the command, not only the last one.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC integ-txn-commit-on-success ===\nIntent: run_command commits the writes of a command that returns normally.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom cli.transactional import run_command\n\ncode = \"CASH1\"\nname = \"Float\"\nwith db_session() as db:\n    def succeeding(store):\n        store.accounts.create(account=code, name=name)\n        return None\n\n    run_command(db, succeeding)\n    rows = db.accounts.list()\n    assert len(rows) == 1\n    assert rows[0].account == code\n    assert rows[0].name == name\n=== END AC integ-txn-commit-on-success ===\n\n=== AC integ-txn-rollback-single-table ===\nIntent: run_command rolls back a write made before the command raises, leaving the table empty.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom cli.transactional import run_command\n\ncode = \"CASH1\"\nname = \"Float\"\nwith db_session() as db:\n    def failing(store):\n        store.accounts.create(account=code, name=name)\n        raise ValueError(\"rejected after write\")\n\n    raised = False\n    try:\n        run_command(db, failing)\n    except ValueError:\n        raised = True\n    assert raised\n    assert len(db.accounts.list()) == 0\n=== END AC integ-txn-rollback-single-table ===\n\n=== AC integ-txn-rollback-multi-table ===\nIntent: run_command rolls back writes to two tables when the command raises after both writes.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom cli.transactional import run_command\n\ncode = \"CASH1\"\nname = \"Float\"\ncategory = \"OPENING\"\ndescription = \"Opening balance\"\nwith db_session() as db:\n    def failing(store):\n        store.accounts.create(account=code, name=name)\n        store.categories.create(category=category, description=description)\n        raise ValueError(\"rejected after two writes\")\n\n    raised = False\n    try:\n        run_command(db, failing)\n    except ValueError:\n        raised = True\n    assert raised\n    assert len(db.accounts.list()) == 0\n    assert len(db.categories.list()) == 0\n=== END AC integ-txn-rollback-multi-table ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- A command that fails leaves every table exactly as it was before the command started.\n- No application-level lock is added around the transaction.\n- Database drivers are never opened here; access goes through the persistence handle only.\n=== END ARTIFACT
